- **cis-linux-hardening**: Harden a Linux endpoint against the CIS Benchmark: audit, remediation and verification of the controls that matter. Use when securing a Debian, Ubuntu or RHEL host. ([ferr079/claude-code-cybersec-skills](https://github.com/ferr079/claude-code-cybersec-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/cis-linux-hardening))
- **cis-local-artifact-retention**: Inventory, preview, compact, retrieve, restore, and clean derived CIS local artifacts. Use when `.cis/local/` grows, before archiving a completed delivery, or when older diagnostic/test evidence must be recovered. ([andrewspiteri/CIS](https://github.com/andrewspiteri/CIS/tree/HEAD/.github/skills/cis-local-artifact-retention))
- **cis-maintain-contracts**: Keep API, data, configuration, package, permission, route, and ERD references synchronized with implementation contract changes. ([andrewspiteri/CIS](https://github.com/andrewspiteri/CIS/tree/HEAD/.github/skills/cis-maintain-contracts))
- **cis-maintain-domain-behaviour**: Keep commands, events, workflow states, invariants, projections, problem details, and module boundaries aligned with behavior changes. ([andrewspiteri/CIS](https://github.com/andrewspiteri/CIS/tree/HEAD/.github/skills/cis-maintain-domain-behaviour))
- **cis-mcp-adapter**: Expose bounded CIS graph, reference, and change context to a local MCP client. Use when configuring an editor or agent to query an initialized repository through stdio, or when reviewing whether a canonical mutation may be exposed. ([andrewspiteri/CIS](https://github.com/andrewspiteri/CIS/tree/HEAD/.github/skills/cis-mcp-adapter))
- **cis-nat-identification-from-genome-annotation**: Use when the task is to comprehensively extract cis-NAT pairs from genome annotation (id, Chr, Strand, Start, End columns, e.g. from Ensembl) with optional RNA-seq expression profiling (TPM, FPKM, fold_change, padj), and to classify them into EB/FO/HH/TT structural types. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cis-nat-identification-from-genome-annotation))
- **cis-nat-overlap-extraction**: Use when a gene annotation (CSV with id, Chr, Strand, Start, End) must be mined for opposite-strand cis-NAT pairs, especially fully-overlapped (FO) pairs where one gene's transcribed interval completely contains its partner's. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cis-nat-overlap-extraction))
- **cis-nat-pair-count-tabulation**: Use when a CCIVR run has produced Table.csv with cis-NAT pairs carrying fold_change and padj expression columns, and you need expression-combination counts (down-down, down-up, up-down, up-up) per EB/FO/HH/TT type or a down-up pair set. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cis-nat-pair-count-tabulation))
- **cis-nat-pair-partner-gene-selection**: Use when you have CCIVR output from an RNA-seq dataset (e.g. Huh-7 cells with TGFβ stimulation) and need to partition differentially expressed cis-NAT partner genes by direction (fold_change/padj) into gene lists for enrichment analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cis-nat-pair-partner-gene-selection))
- **cis-nat-type-frequency-quantification**: Use when genome annotations (e.g. Ensembl GTF releases 105/104 or Ensembl plants/fungi release 52) must be converted and screened with CCIVR to tabulate cis-NAT type counts and percentages per organism. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cis-nat-type-frequency-quantification))

- **cis-policy-impact**: Analyse surfaces governed by a changed policy, standard, ADR, or architecture rule. Use before creating remediation work from governance changes. ([andrewspiteri/CIS](https://github.com/andrewspiteri/CIS/tree/HEAD/.github/skills/cis-policy-impact))
- **cis-regulatory-distance-decay-modeling**: Use when preprocessing paired scRNA-seq/scATAC-seq for cnmf joint clustering and you need a peak-gene coupling matrix that weights open chromatin peaks by distance to genes (d0 = 40 kb decay). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cis-regulatory-distance-decay-modeling))
- **cis-regulatory-motif-enrichment-filtering**: Use when converting co-expression modules into directed gene regulatory networks: after GRNBoost2 proposes candidate TF–TG edges from single-cell expression data, to retain only edges with cis-regulatory motif evidence. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cis-regulatory-motif-enrichment-filtering))
- **cis-release-rollout**: Plan and verify staged release, feature-flag, compatibility, rollback, and post-release checks. Use when a change affects deployment or published packages. ([andrewspiteri/CIS](https://github.com/andrewspiteri/CIS/tree/HEAD/.github/skills/cis-release-rollout))
- **cis-repository-bootstrap**: Create CIS for a new empty project or import an existing repository, respect confirmation gates, and run repository doctor after onboarding errors or collisions. ([andrewspiteri/CIS](https://github.com/andrewspiteri/CIS/tree/HEAD/.github/skills/cis-repository-bootstrap))
